- **Why NOT `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase`.** `FieldLoader.ParseHashSetOrList`/`ParseDictionary` (`engine/OpenRA.Game/FieldLoader.cs:500-536`) build each collection with `Activator.CreateInstance(fieldType, capacity)` — a fresh DEFAULT-comparer instance that discards any comparer set in the field initializer. To swap the comparer you must REPLACE the instance, but these fields are `readonly` (can't reassign without reflection or dropping readonly). Lowercasing the contents needs no reassignment, touches only the flagged collections, and cannot regress ordinal semantics for any other string set (production-queue `Type` sets, target-type `BitSet`s, terrain/cohesion enums stay ordinal).
- **Behavior-identity (both @stable and @experimental, today).** Every flagged field's current YAML values in `mods/ww3mod/rules/ai/{ai,ai-america,ai-russia}.yaml` are all-lowercase (verified field-by-field), so `ToLowerInvariant()` maps each to itself and the normalized collection is element-identical. Consumers compare against always-lowercase actor names (`Ruleset.cs:126`), so all matches are unchanged. The ONLY behavior that changes is a future uppercase value: silent miss → still matches.
- **Fields hardened (RulesetLoaded call site → normalized fields):** `SquadManagerBotModule.cs:107` (AirUnitsTypes, ExcludeFromSquadsTypes, NavalUnitsTypes, IncludeInSquadTypes, ConstructionYardTypes, NavalProductionTypes, ProtectionTypes); `UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:75` (UnitsToBuild, UnitLimits, UnitDelays, ResupplyUnitTypes, AntiAirUnitTypes); `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:246` (CapturingActorTypes, CapturableActorTypes [half-guarded], SupportingUnitTypes, IncomeWeights); `BaseBuilderBotModule.cs:106` (ConstructionYardTypes, VehiclesFactoryTypes, BarracksTypes, HeliTypes, AirfieldTypes, ProductionTypes, NavalProductionTypes, DefenseTypes, BuildingFractions, BuildingLimits, BuildingDelays); `AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:142` (AntiVehicleUnits, AntiInfantryUnits, AntiAirUnits, AirStrikeUnits); `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:392`/`PoiGarrisonBotModule.cs:129`/`LaneAmbushBotModule.cs:110` (ExcludeUnitTypes); `LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs:132` (ScreenUnitTypes, MainLineUnitTypes, ExcludedActorTypes); `SupplyFollowerBotModule.cs:97` (SupplyTruckTypes); `ScoutBotModule.cs:38` (ScoutTypes); `MountedTransportBotModule.cs:117` (CarrierTypes, PassengerTypes [half-guarded]); `GarrisonBotModule.cs:36` (GarrisonActorTypes); `CaptureManagerBotModule.cs:44` (CapturingActorTypes, CapturableActorTypes — legacy, not instantiated, hardened for family completeness).
- **Deliberately LEFT ordinal (would BREAK if lowercased — different namespace, correctly case-sensitive):** production-queue `Type` sets `UnitQueues`/`BuildingQueues`/`DefenseQueues` (`Vehicle`/`Infantry`/`Aircraft`/`Building`/`Defense`); `SquadManager.IgnoredEnemyTargetTypes` (`BitSet<TargetableType>`, tokens `Submarine`/`IgnoreThis`); terrain sets `WaterTerrainTypes`/`CoverTerrainTypes` (`Water`/`Tree`/`Rough`/`Field`); cohesion enums; and the hardcoded lowercase-default `SupplyRouteTypes = {"supplyroute"}` (not user config) across 5 modules.
- **Out of scope (same hazard shape, different trait — NOT a bot module, so outside the audit's boundary): `PoiMap.IncomeWeights`** (`engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/Traits/World/PoiMap.cs:93`, consumed `:212/:348/:415/:483` by `ContainsKey(name)`/`TryGetValue(name)` against actor names; a World trait). Currently lowercase-safe (`world.yaml:309`). Recommended for the same one-line treatment in a follow-up.
- **Coverage:** NUnit `ActorNameCaseTest` (`engine/OpenRA.Test/OpenRA.Mods.Common/ActorNameCaseTest.cs`) pins both mechanisms (uppercase HashSet value + uppercase Dictionary key match a lowercase lookup post-normalize), the behavior-identity/idempotency no-op on all-lowercase input, and null/empty safety. Suite 725/725 (721 baseline + 4 pins). Build clean (`./make.ps1 all`).

## 2026-08-02 — Bot-module actor-name config is a case-mismatch MINEFIELD: 2 confirmed defects (fixed) + ~29 active LATENT fields, all riding one root (`Ruleset.cs:126` lowercases every actor name at load)

> **[promoted (root fact) → conventions.md §Engine behaviors that surprise (same actor-name case pitfall — `GameRules/Ruleset.cs:126` lowercases every actor name, so a case-sensitive config set silently no-matches; the grep tell). The exhaustive latent-field enumeration is superseded by the `ActorNameCase` hardening (entry above) and is ephemeral audit detail, not banked.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Systematic sweep of every bot-module config field in `mods/ww3mod/rules/ai/{ai,ai-america,ai-russia}.yaml` that carries actor-name keys/lists, cross-read against the engine comparison site (not the field name/comment). Static analysis only, no run. Full per-defect evidence + blast radius in [`bugs/discovered.md`](bugs/discovered.md) (2026-08-02 entries).

- **The root cause is ONE fact.** `Ruleset.cs:126` builds every `ActorInfo` with `k.Key.ToLowerInvariant()`, so `actor.Info.Name` is ALWAYS lowercase at runtime. Any bot-module field that stores YAML verbatim in a default `HashSet<string>`/`Dictionary<string,int>` (ordinal, case-SENSITIVE) and then does `Contains(a.Info.Name)` / `ContainsKey(name)` / `b.Name == key` silently NO-MATCHES the moment the YAML value carries an uppercase letter. No warning, no exception — the unit is just never built / never classified / mispriced. **The tell** (grep signature): `= new HashSet<string>()` or `= null` (`Dictionary`) with NO `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase`, consumed by `.Contains(<name>)` / `.ContainsKey(<name>)` / `== <key>`.
- **2 CONFIRMED (both fixed this pass, keys lowercased):** (1) `UnitBuilderBotModule` `UnitsToBuild`/`UnitLimits`/`UnitDelays` — `Dictionary<string,int>` (`:31/:34/:37`), compared at `:327` (`b.Name == unit.Key`), `:191/:195/:200` (`ContainsKey`). UPPERCASE `A10/F16/MIG/FROG/HELI/TRAN/HIND/MI28/HALO` → those aircraft UNBUILDABLE for ALL fog bots (`enable-ai-any`: `@experimental` + `@stable`), both factions. Was the "US only ever buys the littlebird" report. (2) `SquadManagerBotModule.AirUnitsTypes` — case-sensitive `HashSet` (`:31`), read at `:351` (classification, now cured by `UseUnitRoles`) AND UNCONDITIONALLY at `:421/:430` (rush own/enemy-aircraft filters, NOT covered by the role gate). UPPERCASE → own aircraft scooped into ground rushes + AI rushes enemy planes.
- **~29 ACTIVE LATENT fields — case-sensitive, currently lowercase-SAFE, one uppercase edit from silent failure.** All below are set in the ww3 YAML with all-lowercase values today, so they work — but they carry ZERO defense against a future uppercase typo. Highest-severity sub-class is the **`Dictionary`-keyed** ones (silent MISPRICE/skip, exactly the confirmed-#1 mechanism): `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.IncomeWeights` (`:57`, looked up at `:1112` with a lowercased name `:1111` — an uppercase key silently falls to `DefaultIncomeWeight`=10, a capture-priority MISPRICE, not a hard fail), `BaseBuilderBotModule.BuildingLimits` (`:101`, `ContainsKey` at `:202/:268`) and `BuildingFractions` (`:98`). **`HashSet`-Contains** latent fields: `CaptureCoordinator.CapturingActorTypes` (`:35`→`:1282/:1319/:1360`); `BaseBuilder` `ConstructionYardTypes`/`VehiclesFactoryTypes`/`BarracksTypes`/`HeliTypes`/`AirfieldTypes`/`ProductionTypes` (`:22-33`→`:115`, `BaseBuilderQueueManager.cs:199`) + `DefenseTypes` (`:39`→`QueueManager:149`); `AdaptiveProductionBotModule` `AntiVehicleUnits`/`AntiInfantryUnits`/`AntiAirUnits`/`AirStrikeUnits` (`:30-111`→`:579`); `UnitBuilder` `ResupplyUnitTypes`/`AntiAirUnitTypes` (`:53/:67`→`:206/:209/:245`); `SquadManager.ExcludeFromSquadsTypes` (`:34`→`:298/:421/:430`); `PoiOffensiveBotModule.ExcludeUnitTypes` (`:100`→`:1056`); `PoiGarrisonBotModule.ExcludeUnitTypes` (`:89`→`:426`); `LaneAmbushBotModule.ExcludeUnitTypes` (`:104`→`:544`); `LayeredDefenceBotModule` `ScreenUnitTypes`/`MainLineUnitTypes`/`ExcludedActorTypes` (`:52/:56/:90`→`:297/:302/:305/:306`); `SupplyFollowerBotModule.SupplyTruckTypes` (`:25`→`:190/:200`); `ScoutBotModule.ScoutTypes` (`:24`→`:135` via `==`).
- **"Half-guarded" latent — `ToLowerInvariant` on the NAME, but the SET is still case-sensitive.** `CaptureCoordinator.CapturableActorTypes` (`:540/:816/:1247` do `Contains(actor.Info.Name.ToLowerInvariant())`) and `MountedTransportBotModule.CarrierTypes`/`PassengerTypes` (`:188/:413/:466`) lowercase the incoming name — but the HashSet is built from raw YAML, so an uppercase YAML VALUE (e.g. `Bradley`) still misses. The `.ToLowerInvariant()` only defends against a hypothetical un-lowercased actor def; the real requirement remains "YAML values must be lowercase." Same latent risk class, NOT immune.
- **INERT-latent (case-sensitive but the field is UNSET in ww3 YAML today):** `SquadManager` `NavalUnitsTypes`/`ProtectionTypes`/`ConstructionYardTypes`/`IncludeInSquadTypes`, `GarrisonBotModule.GarrisonActorTypes`, and the whole legacy `CaptureManagerBotModule` (not instantiated — `@experimental` folded its role into `CaptureCoordinator`). No live risk, but they'd bite if configured with uppercase later.
- **CLEAN — different namespace, correctly case-sensitive, MUST NOT be lowercased.** These look like the family but compare against engine tokens that are NOT lowercased at load, so their capitalization is CORRECT and a naive "lowercase all AI YAML" fix would BREAK them: production-queue `Type` sets `UnitQueues`/`BuildingQueues`/`DefenseQueues` (`Vehicle`/`Infantry`/`Aircraft`/`Building`/`Defense`); `SquadManager.IgnoredEnemyTargetTypes` (target-type tokens `Submarine`/`IgnoreThis`, matched vs `Targetable.TargetTypes`); cohesion enums `ApproachCohesion`/`AssaultCohesion` (`Spread`/`Tight`); terrain sets `CoverTerrainTypes`/`WaterTerrainTypes` (`Tree`/`Rough`/`Field`/`Water`); and the hardcoded-default `SupplyRouteTypes = {"supplyroute"}` (lowercase, not user config) across 5 modules.
- **DURABLE FIX (deferred to the separate reviewed batch, NOT done here).** Per-field lowercasing (what the two confirmed fixes did) is a whack-a-mole that leaves ~29 landmines armed. The real fix is to construct each actor-name `HashSet`/`Dictionary` with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` at the trait level (`AirUnitsTypes`, the three `UnitBuilder` dicts, `IncomeWeights`, the `BaseBuilder` sets/dicts, `Adaptive*` pools, the `Exclude*`/`*UnitTypes` sets, etc.) so config case can NEVER silently no-op — while explicitly LEAVING the "clean" namespace fields above ordinal. That is an engine change touching `@stable` code paths, so it needs the reviewed-batch + benchmark gate.
## 2026-08-02 — Squad-Brain Phase 4 (strategic-target pinning) landed on `auto/brain-phase4` (from main @ 1c1469a8): the heli FSM's `TargetActor` is BOTH the strategic and the tactical target, so a NEW per-Squad pin field splits them without the (unmerged) Phase-2 Mission object. Key trap: a naive pin loops the FSM forever unless the stall/too-hot transitions RELEASE it.

> **[rejected: branch/design-doc narrative. Squad-Brain Phase 4 strategic-target pinning is implementation of `plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md` §3.3; its home is that design doc. The one general fact (`Squad.StrategicTarget` is transient bot state, not serialized — like `ApproachWaypoint`) is minor and already the documented pattern.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Implements the strategic-target-pinning slice of Phase 4 from [`plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md`](plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md) §3.3/§1.3. Pure-math + FSM wiring + NUnit only; no game run (unit-test scope). Files: `Squad.cs` (two pin fields), `HelicopterStates.cs` (base-state helpers + `HeliMissionPinMath` + 5 FSM integration points), `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs` (2 Info fields), `ai.yaml` @experimental heli block, `HeliMissionPinMathTest.cs`.

- **The heli squad has NO Mission object to pin to — Phase 2 is unmerged (parallel branch), so the pin lives on `Squad` directly.** Root cause C (§1.3) is that `Squad.TargetActor` is simultaneously the strategic and the micro target, so 5-tick predicate flapping IS strategic churn. The fix adds `Squad.StrategicTarget` (+ `StrategicCommitTick`) as the durable strategic objective, kept separate from the tactical `TargetActor`; the FSM re-aims `TargetActor` on every micro-transition but only the abort triggers touch the pin. This is the heli-squad analogue of `Mission.Objective` and migrates into `SquadBrainBotModule` when Phase 2 lands. **The pin is NOT serialized** — like the pre-existing `Squad.ApproachWaypoint` (`Squad.cs:36`), it is transient bot state, so the save format is byte-identical and a loaded squad resumes unpinned and re-acquires.
- **TRAP — a naive pin makes the FSM loop forever.** The existing `HelicopterApproachState` safeties (`stuckTicks > 200 → Idle` `:614`, and too-hot-no-soft `→ Withdraw`) hand back to a state that, WITH a pin, re-resumes the SAME objective (Idle now routes to the pin instead of re-picking, `:374`). An unreachable / unassailable objective would then cycle Approach→Idle→Approach or Approach→Withdraw→Approach indefinitely. So the pin MUST be released at exactly those two transitions: trigger 6 clears it inside the `stuckTicks > 200` block (`:621`), trigger 2 (N5) clears it in the too-hot-no-soft `else` when the too-hot target IS the objective (`:506-509`, `ReferenceEquals(TargetActor, StrategicTarget)`). Missing either re-introduces the very dither the pin removes, one level up.
- **The heli FSM can feed only a SUBSET of the §3.3 abort triggers — the rest need the Brain.** Pinned-lease check `HeliMissionPinMath.EvaluatePin` (`:880`) covers trigger 1 (objective invalid — a live-actor validity read mirroring `FindClosestEnemy`'s filter, `StrategicTargetValid` `:198`) + the bounded-window backstop; `ObjectiveTooHotAbort` (`:896`) covers the heli form of trigger 2 (objective-itself-too-hot-no-divert, §3.3 N5) reusing the FSM's existing `IsTargetTooHot`; trigger 6 (stall) reuses the FSM's existing `stuckTicks`. Route-danger-spike (full trigger 2) and better-opportunity (trigger 3) need the `DangerFieldLayer` route sample and rival scores — deliberately deferred to `SquadBrainBotModule`, not bolted onto this executor-local pin.
- **Byte-identity: only the flag-OFF path is constrained, and it is trivially unchanged.** Every pin read/write is guarded by `StrategicTargetPinningEnabled(owner)` (`:180`, an Info-bool lookup mirroring `StandoffEngagementEnabled`); with it false, `Squad.StrategicTarget` is never written and each state's target logic collapses to the frozen expression. The flag is set ONLY in `HelicopterSquadBotModule@experimental` (`ai.yaml`); `@stable` OMITS it and takes the engine default `false` — the SAME "parity copy but the mission-commitment experimental flags are deliberately not copied" pattern the offense module's `MissionCommitmentEnabled` uses (`@stable` block `ai.yaml:1028-1068` has no such field). Per-profile trait instance ⇒ the single default-off flag suffices (influence-stack gating pattern 1); no `Participates`-gate needed since this is not a shared-module consumer.
- **Tick source:** `Squad.World` is `internal`, `World.WorldTick` is `public` (`World.cs:442`) — the state classes are same-assembly/same-namespace so `owner.World.WorldTick` is the deterministic commit-tick stamp; no RNG anywhere in the pin path. NUnit **731/731** (721 baseline + 10 pins). `./make.ps1 all` 0 errors.
## 2026-08-02 — Squad-Brain Phase 2 executor commit-on-order audit landed on `auto/brain-phase2` (from main @ 1bae9628): the four known ledger-blind support/passenger/garrison/line writers now commit every unit they order. Key finding: the doc's fifth item — "BuildFreePool ignoring IsPassengerReserved" — needs NO BuildFreePool edit; it is SUBSUMED by the MountedTransport commit-on-load (BuildFreePool already excludes ledger-committed units).

> **[promoted (core invariant) → architecture.md §The PoiGoalGuard commitment ledger (commit-on-order is a PAIR: commit every ordered unit AND recruit only from the ledger-checked pool, else a poach; disjoint objective-key grammar). Verified `PoiGoalGuard.cs:285-300` + the key prefixes across the writer modules. The per-writer audit findings are branch detail whose home is the design doc.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Implements the §4 / Phase-2 executor-by-executor commit audit from [`plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md`](plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md) (the audit slice only — NOT the `Mission`/`SquadBrainBotModule` objects). Commit-on-order is the coexistence invariant (§4 FIX 3): every executor commits every unit it orders, at order time, AND recruits only from the ledger-checked free pool — both halves are required, since commit-without-recruit-check still lets a writer poach a unit another already committed (see the follow-up MountedTransport fixes below). Pure-math + wiring + NUnit only; no game/autotest run. Files: `PoiGoalGuard.cs` (new `CommitOnOrderMath`), `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs`, `MountedTransportBotModule.cs`, `GarrisonBotModule.cs`, `LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs`, `ai.yaml` (@experimental / shared blocks only), new `CommitOnOrderMathTest.cs`.

- **The ledger runs for BOTH fog-legal bots, so a bare "does the ledger exist" gate is INSUFFICIENT.** `PoiGoalGuard@poi` is gated `enable-ai-experimental || enable-ai-stable` (`ai.yaml:84`) — the @stable bot carries the ledger too (post stable-0802). So committing whenever `goalGuard != null` would change @stable behaviour (its recruitment pool shrinks). Every commit is therefore behind a **default-off per-module flag, set true ONLY in the @experimental block**, mirroring the landed `RespectCommitmentLedger`/`MissionCommitmentEnabled` pattern. Because the ledger itself is @experimental/@stable-gated and every touched writer is a per-profile twin (except Garrison, below), the flag needs NO new `!enable-ai-experimental` clause and NO new YAML on any @stable twin. The gate seam is the new pure `CommitOnOrderMath.ShouldCommit(flag, ledgerAvailable)` (`PoiGoalGuard.cs:292`).
- **AUDIT FINDING 1 — CaptureCoordinator escorts + defenders (CONFIRMED uncommitted, now fixed).** `IssueCaptureOrder` commits only the capturer (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:986`). `DispatchEscort` recruits from the ledger-checked pool (`FindIdleSupportersNear` checks `IsCommitted` `:1322`), issues the grouped `AttackMove` (`:1196`), adds to `alreadyRecruited` (`:1198`) — but never committed. `QueueDefenseOrders` recruits defenders, orders them (`:1293`), stamps a **bespoke `defenderBookings` dict** (`:1294-95`) that only THIS module honours — invisible to offense/LayeredDefence. Fix: commit escorts under `capture-escort:<targetId>` (`:1222-26`) and defenders under `capture-defend:<structureId>` (`:1329-33`), behind `CommitSupportUnits` (`:256`, `ai.yaml` @experimental.tecn). Release is via the shared ledger TTL/Prune (the capturer's own backstop). Disjoint keys keep both attributable and distinct from PoiGarrison's `defend:`.
- **AUDIT FINDING 2 — MountedTransport (CONFIRMED ledger-blind, now fixed).** Zero ledger refs; its only cross-module lock was the bespoke `IsPassengerReserved` seam (`:155`), which LayeredDefence honours (`LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs:324`) but offense's `BuildFreePool` does NOT. Fix: commit frontline-delivery passengers under `transport:<carrierId>` on load (`CommitTaskPassengers` `:186`, called at `:591`), release on unload/teardown (`ReleaseTaskPassengers` at `:348/:374/:419/:443`), behind `CommitPassengers` (`:125`, @experimental). The capture-ferry path is deliberately NOT committed here (its TECN is already `capture:<id>` and not in-world while aboard). **Follow-up (adversarial review of `870353f2`, both @experimental-only, fixed in the next commit):** (a) **carrier-death leaked commitments** — the stale-carrier teardown loop `Remove`d dead/departed carrier tasks WITHOUT `ReleaseTaskPassengers`, unlike the other four teardown paths, so a carrier killed mid-Loading left its alive passengers ledger-locked under `transport:<deadCarrierId>` for the full 300-tick TTL; now releases before remove. (b) **passenger selection was NOT ledger-aware** — `availablePassengers` filtered only the bespoke `reservedByOthers` set, never `Ledger.IsCommitted`, so transport could recruit a unit already committed by offense/defence/garrison and the `Commit()` on load OVERWROTE the prior objective (a poach the review correctly flagged against the "commits ⇒ no poach" claim); now adds `!goalGuard.Ledger.IsCommitted(a, tick)` to the predicate (the `GarrisonBotModule.cs:154` pattern). The lesson: commit-on-order alone is insufficient without a recruit-from-free-pool check on the SAME writer — the two are a pair.
- **AUDIT FINDING 5 (BuildFreePool) — REFUTED as a separate edit; subsumed.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule.BuildFreePool` (`:1017`, drifted from the doc's `:878-890`) ALREADY excludes ledger-committed units (`:1025`). Once MountedTransport commits its passengers (finding 2), that existing check excludes them — exactly the doc's own "passengers … ledger-committed … replacing the bespoke `IsPassengerReserved` cross-check" intent. Adding a redundant `IsPassengerReserved` probe would force PoiOffensive to resolve the transport module for no behavioural gain in the shipped config and against the single-lock architecture. So the fix is the transport commit, not a BuildFreePool change. Committing passengers in the SAME synchronous pass as the `EnterTransport` order (no select→commit gap) means no residual steal window.
- **AUDIT FINDING 3 — GarrisonBotModule@defenses (CONFIRMED ledger-blind, now fixed) — the ONLY shared writer, needs a runtime BotType gate.** Uses `BotBlackboard.ClaimUnit`/`IsClaimedByOtherModule` (`:181/:184`), not the ledger. It is a SINGLE `enable-ai-any` instance (`ai.yaml:467`) whose per-player twin runs for @experimental, @stable, Normal AND legacy — so a per-profile YAML flag can't confine it. Per §6, the gate adds an explicit `player.BotType == InfluenceStack.ExperimentalBotType` term: new `CommitOnOrderMath.ShouldCommitShared(flag, ledger, isExperimentalBot)` (`PoiGoalGuard.cs:300`), surfaced as `LedgerActive` (`GarrisonBotModule.cs:66`). Both the free-pool read (`:154`) and the commit under `garrison:<buildingId>` (`:189-90`) fire only for the @experimental player; Normal/legacy have no `PoiGoalGuard` at all (double safety). `CommitGarrisonedUnits` (`:43`) set on the single shared block; @stable/Normal/legacy stay byte-identical.
- **AUDIT FINDING 4 — LayeredDefence write-side (CONFIRMED read-only, now writes).** `1fec5070` landed the READ side (`RespectCommitmentLedger`, skips committed units `:331`) but the assignment only stamps a private `assignedAtTick` (`:425`) — never a ledger commit, leaving the reverse steal channel (offense grabbing an idle line unit, N6) open. Fix: commit each line/screen slot under `defend-line:<x>,<y>` (`:446`) with **TTL = `AssignCooldownTicks`** (250), so the ledger claim expires exactly when this module's own anti-thrash cooldown does — the line re-flows on one clock while other writers defer for the window. Behind `CommitLineAssignments` (`:131`, @experimental); `goalGuard` now resolved when either flag is on (`:180`).
- **No ADDITIONAL uncommitted combat-pool writers found.** Swept all BotModules: PoiOffensive commits (`:1112`), PoiGarrison reads+commits (`:403/:460`), LaneAmbush commits (`:416`), the four fixed-wing SquadManagers own no ground (`IgnoreGroundUnits`), Heli is air-squad-membership only, SupplyFollower moves only `truk` (no `AttackBase` ⇒ never in the combat free pool). So the doc's known three + LayeredDefence write-side is the COMPLETE set for the ground pool.
- **Disjoint objective-key grammar (audit req (d)) — verified.** New keys `capture-escort:` / `capture-defend:` / `transport:` / `garrison:` / `defend-line:` are pairwise-distinct prefixes and disjoint from the pre-existing `capture:` / `offense:` / `defend:` / `ambush:`; the line key is CELL-based (not actor-based) so it can never collide with an actor-id key. Pinned in `CommitOnOrderMathTest.DisjointKeys_IndependentCommitmentsCoexist`.
- **Byte-identity gate.** All five flags default-off on the engine class; four are absent on their @stable twins; the shared Garrison flag is neutralised for non-experimental players by the runtime BotType term. No new field on any @stable block. When every flag is off, each commit/read collapses to the pre-change expression (the ledger reference resolves null). NUnit **727/727** (721 baseline + 6 new commit-on-order pins: the 2 gate truth tables, the steal-window-closed/open pair, disjoint-keys, transport-release). `./make.ps1 all` 0 errors.

## 2026-08-02 — Squad-Brain Phase 1c/1d landed on `auto/brain-1c` (from main @ cd396526): believed-field score quantization for the mission trigger-3 rival compare + Aggressiveness slider scaffolding. Key finding: magnitude quantization CANNOT erase a genuine single-bucket score step — it only damps the bucket-EDGE rival wobble; the committed-cell danger collapse is (correctly) trigger 2's job.

> **[rejected: branch/design-doc implementation detail. Phase 1c/1d score quantization (`QuantizeAxisScore` relative-band floor) + the inert Aggressiveness slider are implementation of `plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md` §7/§3.3; ships inert (bandPct 0 / slope 0). No durable general fact beyond the design doc.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Implements Phase 1c (`QuantizeAxisScore`) + 1d (slider infra) from [`plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md`](plans/260802_squad_brain_design.md). Pure-math + wiring + NUnit only; no game run (unit-test scope). Files: `PoiGoalGuard.cs` (MissionCommitmentMath), `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs` (PoiOffenseMath + PartitionHeldAxes + Info fields), `ai.yaml` @experimental, two test files.

- **Design decision — quantization is CALLER-SIDE, helper co-located in MissionCommitmentMath.** The open call (doc §7 Q1) was: quantize inside PoiGoalGuard, or caller-side in the offense module? Resolution keeps BOTH virtues: the pure helpers `QuantizeAxisScore(long,long)` + `BetterOpportunityQuantized(committed,alt,marginPct,bandPct)` live in `MissionCommitmentMath` (so they port verbatim to the v3 Brain's trigger 3, §3.3), but the DECISION to quantize is caller-side — `PartitionHeldAxes` passes `Info.MissionScoreQuantizeBandPct` into a NEW 16-arg `ShouldReassign` overload; the legacy 15-arg overload delegates with band pct `0`. `bandPct ≤ 0` collapses `BetterOpportunityQuantized` VERBATIM to raw `BetterOpportunity`, so every existing consumer + NUnit pin of the raw predicates is byte-identical. No other consumer of any score changed.
- **Relative (not absolute) band — forced by the score dynamic range.** Offense `Score = value·distanceFactor·threatFactor·ownershipMul` (`PoiMap.cs:662`, each factor ×100-scaled) then ×believed-field `mul/100`, spanning ~1e8–1e12 depending on POI value. An ABSOLUTE integer band (the codebase's usual threshold style, e.g. `SafeControlScoreThreshold:300`) cannot be "coarse" across that whole range and would overflow `int`. So the band is RELATIVE: `band = max(committed,alt)·bandPct/100`, floor both. Scale-invariant; a rival must be ≥ ~1/(1−bandPct/100) × the committed score to jump a band (bandPct 60 ⇒ ~1.67×). This effectively makes bandPct the bucket-aware material-improvement gate, reinforcing `MissionBetterOppMarginPct`.
- **FLOOR, not round.** Flooring snaps the wobbling UPPER value DOWN into the committed axis's band — exactly the suppression wanted for a rival hovering just above the margin. Rounding can push the upper value UP across a band boundary and re-introduce the flip (verified by hand: committed 1000, rival 990↔1650, bandPct 60 → floor HOLDS both states, round FLIPS).
- **HONEST LIMIT (doc-vs-code nuance for the adversarial review).** §3.3/FIX 7 says quantization makes "a bucket-edge wobble ... cannot clear the margin." That is TRUE for the class the review's example shows — a RIVAL cell wobbling across a believed-danger bucket edge (both scores land in a common band ⇒ HOLD). It is NOT achievable, by construction, for a genuine single-bucket step on the COMMITTED cell (danger 60→20 ⇒ its score ×⅓): no magnitude quantizer can distinguish that from a real 3× change, and it SHOULD reassign — that collapse is a real danger rise the mission would grind into, caught by **trigger 2** (danger-spike delta hysteresis), which is the designated damper. So 1c narrows/kills the trigger-3 rival-wobble ping-pong; it does not (and must not) freeze a mission whose own objective became a kill-zone.
- **`same-kind only` (FIX 7) is already satisfied structurally in offense — no code change needed.** Within `PoiOffensiveBotModule`, every entry in `targets` (and thus `BestAlternativeScore`, `PoiGoalGuard.cs`… `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:939`) is an offensive POI scored under the one offense factor stack. There is no cross-kind (Defend vs Assault) comparison here to guard; that concern belongs to the future Brain comparing across modules (Phase 2+). Flagged so a reviewer doesn't expect a same-kind filter in 1c.
- **Doc-vs-code naming drift.** The design doc repeatedly calls the aggregate predicate `MissionCommitmentMath.ShouldRelease` (§1.3, §5 1b, §5 seams); the landed code names it `ShouldReassign` (`PoiGoalGuard.cs:187`). No `ShouldRelease` exists. Cosmetic, but the design should be re-stamped or the code renamed to stop the drift widening.
- **1d slider scaffolding ships INERT.** `PoiOffenseMath.ShiftByKnob(base,knob,slopePct)=base+(knob−50)·slopePct/100`; `Aggressiveness` (default 50) threads through it to shift `MissionBetterOppMarginPct` in `PartitionHeldAxes`. The slope `MissionBetterOppMarginSlopePct` defaults to **0** (engine class) and is set to **0** in @experimental, so the knob cannot move any threshold until a sweep sets a nonzero slope — byte-identical regardless of Aggressiveness value. Landed on `PoiOffensiveBotModuleInfo` (first Phase-1 consumer); migrates to `SquadBrainBotModuleInfo` in Phase 2 per §2.7.
- **Byte-identity gate.** `MissionScoreQuantizeBandPct=0`, `Aggressiveness=50`, `MissionBetterOppMarginSlopePct=0` on the engine class; `PartitionHeldAxes` only runs under `MissionCommitmentEnabled` (default-off, absent on the `@stable` twin). No new field added to any `@stable` block. NUnit **710/710** (700 baseline + 10: 3 `ShiftByKnob`, 7 quantization incl. the review's bucket-crossing ping-pong pin). `dotnet build engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common -c Release` 0 errors. `--check-yaml` not runnable here (SDK stages `OpenRA.Mods.Cnc.dll` into `engine/bin` only via `make all` under the .NET 6 runtime; unrelated to the edit) — YAML↔C# field-name parity confirmed by grep instead.
## 2026-08-02 — Mission-commitment timers are a THREE-tier ordering: ReevaluateInterval (100) < AxisCommitmentTicks (250) < MissionCommitmentWindowTicks (400); violate the first inequality and abort triggers can never fire inside a commitment window (auto/commit-tuning, off main @ a9f32bcc)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §The PoiGoalGuard commitment ledger (three-tier timer ordering `ReevaluateInterval` 100 < `AxisCommitmentTicks` 250 < `MissionCommitmentWindowTicks`; window default 0 = inert on the engine class, `@experimental` 400; violate the first inequality and the ledger lapses between re-evals so abort triggers can't fire). Verified `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:57/:87/:437/:636-640/:1245`, `PoiGoalGuard.cs:243/:253-254`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Found while wiring `MissionCommitmentWindowTicks` non-zero (it shipped at 0 = inert with the mission-commitment merge). Documenting the ordering invariant the three PoiOffensive timers must satisfy, since it is NOT self-evident from any single field and the abort-trigger machinery silently degenerates if it is broken. Static-code + YAML analysis (config/doc batch, no game run).

- **The abort triggers are only tested at re-eval ticks.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule` is an `IBotTick` that early-returns until a countdown hits 0, then runs `Reevaluate` once every `ReevaluateInterval` ticks (`PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:479-483`, default 100 `:57`). Mission commitment — the per-axis HOLD/RELEASE decision — is evaluated ONLY inside that re-eval, via `PartitionHeldAxes` → `MissionCommitmentMath.ShouldReassign` (`:898-904`). So a "commitment window" is not continuous time; it is a sequence of discrete re-eval sample points 100 ticks apart. Anything that must fire "inside the window" can only fire AT one of those points.
- **The ledger claim is refreshed every re-eval, and that is what the first inequality protects.** Each re-eval a HELD axis re-asserts its `PoiGoalGuard` ledger claim with a fresh `AxisCommitmentTicks` TTL (`:915`, mirror at first-order `:1081`; default 250 `:87`). The claim is what makes a committed unit invisible to capture/defense/other axes. Because the claim is renewed at +100 and +200 before it would lapse at +250, the unit stays CONTINUOUSLY committed between re-evals. **If `ReevaluateInterval >= AxisCommitmentTicks`, the claim expires in the gap between two re-evals** → the unit is released mid-mission (visible again, re-tasked by the reshuffle) BEFORE the next re-eval can test any abort trigger → the commitment window collapses to zero and the mission-level HOLD/RELEASE triggers (danger spike / better-opportunity / combat-ineffective) can never gate release. The engine `[Desc]` states the bare rule ("must exceed ReevaluateInterval", `:84-86`); this is the WHY.
- **The window is a THIRD, outer tier — set above `AxisCommitmentTicks`, not below.** `MissionCommitmentWindowTicks` (now 400, `ai.yaml` @experimental block; engine default 0 = inert, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:340`) is the bounded safety re-plan valve: `ShouldReassign` force-releases a held axis once `currentTick - commitTick >= commitWindowTicks`, even if no trigger fired (`PoiGoalGuard.cs:197-198`). For the valve to be a BACKSTOP rather than the primary release, it must span multiple re-evals (so triggers get several chances first) AND sit above the ledger TTL (so the axis is held continuously right up to the backstop). Full ordering: **`ReevaluateInterval` (100) < `AxisCommitmentTicks` (250) < `MissionCommitmentWindowTicks` (400)** — re-eval faster than the ledger lapses, ledger lapse-point before the safety valve. 400 gives ~3 held re-evals (+100/+200/+300) then a mandatory re-plan at the +400 sample. A `commitWindowTicks <= 0` disables the valve entirely (pure-trigger hold; objective completion is trigger 1) — the shipped-inert state before this change.
- **@stable byte-identity:** all three fields live only on `PoiOffensiveBotModule@experimental`; the `@stable` twin omits them and takes the engine defaults (`MissionCommitmentWindowTicks = 0`, `MissionCommitmentEnabled = false` `:334`), so its whole partition path is the early `return null` (`:862`) — byte-identical.

## 2026-08-02 — ShadowLayer/DensityLayer are BAKED at map load: tree death does NOT update forest concealment or damage-cover (re-verified at main @ 9392540c; PIPELINE item 20 closed)

> **[promoted: the core BAKED fact was already banked via the 2026-07-28 entry below → architecture.md §Regenerating shadows.bin; this re-verification confirms it unchanged at a newer HEAD and adds the one durable new consumer — the damage-reduction cover (`DensityModifiesDamage` samples the frozen `DensityLayer`), now folded into that section's Consequence line. Verified this pass against `Map.cs:469-509`, `Building.cs:372-397`, `DensityModifiesDamage.cs:72-87`. The two-tier incremental-update feasibility sketch stays in `recon/260802-shadowlayer-tree-death.md` — speculative design, not reference-stable.]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Re-verification of the [`260728-shadowlayer-tree-death.md`](recon/260728-shadowlayer-tree-death.md) verdict against current HEAD (20 commits newer). Full evidence + incremental-update feasibility sketch in [`recon/260802-shadowlayer-tree-death.md`](recon/260802-shadowlayer-tree-death.md). Static-code-analysis only (no build/game/test — the verdict is dispositive without a run).

- **VERDICT unchanged: BAKED.** `Map.DensityLayer` (source, per-cell `byte`) and `Map.ShadowLayer` (derived, per cell-pair) are written only at load — from `shadows.bin` (`Map.cs:469-495`) or the null-fallback `SetDensityLayer`/`SetShadowLayer` (`Map.cs:501-509`) — plus the dead `UpdateDensityForBuilding`. An exhaustive write-site grep across `engine/` returns nothing else; **no `INotifyKilled`/`INotifyRemovedFromWorld`/tick path mutates them.**
- **The one hook a dying tree fires is inert.** Trees are `Building`-derived; death → husk (`SpawnActorOnDeath`) → `Building.RemovedFromWorld` (`Building.cs:386-398`), but its density-decrement + shadow-requeue block is **commented out** (`:392-397`, disabled 260503 for mid-game lag, comment `:372-377`). The mutators `UpdateShadowForCells`/`QueueShadowUpdate`/`FlushPendingShadowUpdates`/`UpdateDensityForBuilding` (`Map.cs:1013-1082/:1183-1203`) are all `CURRENTLY UNUSED (260503)`; their only callers are the commented `Building.cs:381-382/:395-396` lines + the commented `World.cs:508` flush.
- **Even the fallback compute never sees battlefield state:** `SetDensityLayer` iterates `ActorDefinitions` (authored map.yaml actors, `Map.cs:984`), not live `World` actors. Regen (`--regen-shadows`, editor save via `SaveShadowsBinaryData`) rebuilds from the same authored list and never runs mid-match.
- **Case-01 consequence:** shelling/burning a treeline to husks opens NO sightline and removes NO cover — defenders keep full concealment (`MapLayers.cs:365`), item-26 damage reduction (`DensityModifiesDamage.cs:83`), and item-21 concealment scoring on frozen density. Treat the treeline as PERMANENT cover; any "shell trees → they light up" test silently passes on stale data.
- **Incremental-update feasibility (if ever made dynamic):** two tiers. CHEAP — the damage-cover + concealment-scoring consumers read `DensityLayer` directly/per-access, so re-enabling only the `Building.RemovedFromWorld` density decrement makes them live for O(footprint) integer writes, zero shadow recompute. EXPENSIVE — vision/FiringLOS read the derived per-pair `ShadowLayer`, so opening a sightline needs `RecomputeShadowFrom` (full/annulus rebuild = the 260503 lag source; a not-yet-written "pairs whose line crosses cell C" local-window rebuild is the cheapest option). Lockstep risk is **baseline divergence** (stale-vs-regen `shadows.bin` across clients), not the update math (pure integer; audit only the airborne `Ceiling(float)` term).

## 2026-08-02 — Heli corner-idle is a THREE-part park (production rally → SR + full-ammo squad gate + engine `AmmoPool` auto-evac EXCLUDES aircraft); fixed for @experimental with mission-employment evac-to-reserves (auto/heli-missions)

> **[promoted (partial): the durable ENGINE facts → architecture.md §AI configuration. Most were already banked — `ProductionFromMapEdge` no-rally park-at-SR, the `IsReadyForMission`/`SquadHasAmmo` full-ammo gates + `SkipRearmReadyCheck`, and the `AutoRearmIfAllEmpty`→`Evacuate`→`RotateToEdge` path (architecture.md:325/:327); the sell/refund formula was already in economy.md §Cash flow. The one durable NEW fact promoted this pass: `AmmoPool` auto-evac hard-EXCLUDES aircraft (`AmmoPool.cs:173/:247`), so a spent heli has no engine evac path and `FlyIdle`s draining upkeep (`InfersUpkeep.cs:83-89`) until explicitly evac'd — added to the out-of-ammo-evac paragraph. Fixed a stale ref in that section (`IsReadyForMission` `:408`→`:607`, `HasFullAmmo` `:638`). Verified `AmmoPool.cs:170-176/:243-255`, `ProductionFromMapEdge.cs:173-175`, `RotateToEdge.cs:62/:143/:280`, `CustomSellValue.cs:28-55`, `InfersUpkeep.cs:83-89`, `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:607/:638`. The @experimental `EvacuateWhenIdle` lever (now merged @ a9f32bcc) + its adversarial-review FIX-1/FIX-2 + tuning values (500-tick window / 60-cell range / 12-cell home radius) are NOT promoted — a fresh, un-benchmarked behavioural lever whose knobs the entry itself flags as first-guess; left here as branch record until benchmarked.]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Root-caused (code-only, no game run) the "all helis fly to a corner ~4-5 tiles in and idle forever, both teams" playtest bug, then added the exit half of hit-and-run heli employment for @experimental. Base `main` @ `493f76a4`. NOT merged.

- **The "corner" is a REAL cell (the SR beachhead), not a degenerate `(0,0)`.** A called-in aircraft with no `RallyPoint.Path` gets exactly ONE order at spawn: a `Move` to `self.Location` — the Supply Route building's own cell (`ProductionFromMapEdge.cs:173-175, 194-195`; SR sets no default Path per structures.yaml). The SR is a fixed corner/edge beachhead inset a few cells, so the heli flies there and goes idle. Every `CPos.Zero` fallback in the heli path is guarded (`HelicopterIdleState` gates `weakCell != CPos.Zero`, `HelicopterStates.cs:328`; transport drop-zone `:526`), so there is no buggy coordinate to patch — the bug is behavioural (no mission loop), confirming the 2026-07-21 `discovered.md` trace.
- **Why it never re-issues (non-experimental):** `HelicopterSquadBotModule.IsReadyForMission` requires every `AmmoPool.HasFullAmmo` (`:566-577`); WW3MOD attack helis rearm only at an hpad and the mod builds none, so a heli that fired once can never become ready → `TryLaunchAttackMission` never assembles the 2-unit squad (`:397-399`) → the FSM that issues attack/move orders (`HelicopterStates.cs`, `SquadType.Helicopter`→`HelicopterIdleState`, `Squad.cs:66-67`) never runs. The base block `HelicopterSquadBotModule` (ai.yaml:749-757, used by @stable + normal) omits `SkipRearmReadyCheck`, so this gate is LIVE there. @experimental (`ai.yaml:762`) sets `SkipRearmReadyCheck: true` + `ForwardStaging`, so its squads DO form/mission — but between missions / when spent / when target-less, helis still park.
- **ENGINE FACT — `AmmoPool` auto-evac explicitly EXCLUDES aircraft.** `AmmoPool.AutoRearmIfAllEmpty` hard-returns on `self.Info.HasTraitInfo<AircraftInfo>()` (`AmmoPool.cs:173`), and the `INotifyAttack`/`INotifyBecomingIdle` hooks that call it also exclude aircraft (`:247, :254`). So the `ResupplyBehavior.Evacuate` stance that auto-rotates a spent GROUND unit to the map edge (`:197-205` → `new RotateToEdge(self, true, GetSellValue())`) never fires for helis. Helis therefore have NO evac path at all — a spent heli `ReturnToBase`s and (no hpad/Reservable) `FlyIdle`s in place forever, draining upkeep. This is the mechanical reason "evacuate-when-idle" had to be added, not just enabled.
- **Evac mechanics VERIFIED in code (economy-doc formula matches).** `RotateToEdge(self, showTicks, refundAmount)` (`RotateToEdge.cs:62`) flies the actor to the nearest edge, and for aircraft despawns only 5 cells past the boundary (`AircraftOffMapCells`, anti-missile-whoosh). Refund = `fixedRefund × HP/MaxHP` via `playerResources.ChangeCash` (`:270-289`). `refundAmount = self.GetSellValue()` = `Cost − Σ_pools(floor(missingRounds/ReloadCount) × SupplyValue)` (`CustomSellValue.cs:26-55`) ⇒ full ammo refunds full Cost, empty refunds Cost minus spent-ammo value (Hellfire 200/missile). Upkeep stops on `self.Dispose()` via `InfersUpkeep.RemovedFromWorld → RemoveFromUpkeep` (`InfersUpkeep.cs:83-89`; drain is `PlayerResources.Tick … −Upkeep`, `:202`). So evac genuinely banks money AND ends the drain.
- **FIX (auto/heli-missions, @experimental-only, default-off ⇒ @stable/normal byte-identical):** new `HelicopterSquadBotModule.EvacuateWhenIdle` (+ `EvacuateIdleTicks` 500, `EvacuateHomeRadiusCells` 12, `MissionTargetRangeCells` 60). `EvaluateIdleHelicopters()` runs each `BotTick`, iterates managed attack helis in **ActorID order** (HashSet iteration is non-deterministic — must sort for lockstep), and for a genuinely `IsIdle` heli (never one mid-mission/mid-withdraw/mid-evac) evacuates it via `heli.QueueActivity(false, new RotateToEdge(heli, true, heli.GetSellValue()))` when the pure `HeliEmploymentMath.Decide` says so: (1) spent with no rearm host ⇒ evac at once; (2) armed but no BELIEVED worthwhile target (`BeliefStore.Contacts`, fog-legal) AND loitering within the home radius past the patience window ⇒ evac; else HOLD for the squad mission loop. The active mission arc (belief-routed ingress via `HeliDangerNav`, standoff strike, `HitAndRunCooldown`, RTB-on-ammo/damage, withdraw-on-AA-spike) ALREADY existed and is enabled on @experimental — the only missing piece was the exit-to-reserves.
- **The evac MUST be made uncancellable (adversarial-review FIX-1).** `Evacuate` drops the heli from the idle pool / squads / managed set / blackboard — but removing it from `managedHelicopters` is exactly what makes `FindNewHelicopters` (which filters `!managedHelicopters.Contains`) RE-ADOPT it on the next `ScanInterval` (100 ticks), and `IsReadyForMission` rejected only the literal `"Resupply"` activity — so an evacuating heli (`RotateToEdge`) was re-recruited within ~100 ticks and a squad order cancelled its evac (upkeep never stopped; it then fought without ammo). Fixed with a module-tracked `evacuating` `HashSet<Actor>`: the heli is added the moment evac is queued, and BOTH `FindNewHelicopters` and `IsReadyForMission` exclude the set; it is dropped once the heli leaves the world (`RotateToEdge` disposes it at the edge). Engine-blessed alternative signals exist (`RotateToEdge` grants an `"evacuating"` external condition `:143-145`, or `CurrentActivity is RotateToEdge`) but the module set is authoritative from the exact instant of queueing (no `OnFirstRun` grant gap) and is trivially deterministic. Empty set when the flag is off ⇒ both exclusions are no-ops ⇒ byte-identical.
- **Pre-contact evac is gated on first contact (adversarial-review FIX-2).** `EvacuateHomeRadiusCells` (12) > `ForwardStagingMaxDistanceCells` (8), so a forward-staged, target-less heli is inside the evac-eligible home radius — without a gate it would be evac'd and re-bought during the opening before any enemy is seen. A module `enemyEverObserved` latch (true once `BeliefStore.Contacts` is ever non-empty) is threaded into `HeliEmploymentMath.Decide` and gates ONLY the target-less branch; the spent-with-no-rearm branch stays unconditional.
- **Determinism:** zero RNG (existing module RNG at `:397/:468` left untouched), integer geometry (`AnyTargetWithin` squared-distance compare), ActorID-sorted iteration, `HashSet.Contains`/`RemoveWhere` (membership/predicate — order-independent), boolean `world.Actors.Any` for the rearm-host check.
- **Gating / @stable byte-identity:** the EvacuateWhenIdle fields are set ONLY on the `HelicopterSquadBotModule@experimental` block (its own per-profile trait instance — first gating pattern, influence-stack.md §Invariants). The `@stable` twin sets none of them, so they default off regardless of which block it uses. (Correction to an earlier draft of this entry: a `HelicopterSquadBotModule@stable` block DOES exist on current `main` — `ai.yaml:775`, `SkipRearmReadyCheck: true`, from the stable-0802 parity rewrite; this branch is based off `493f76a4` where `@stable` instead inherits the base block. Either way `@stable` never sets EvacuateWhenIdle, and the two shared-code exclusions above are inert with an empty `evacuating` set ⇒ `@stable` is byte-identical.)
- **Verification:** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **633/633** (622 baseline + 11 new `HeliEmploymentMathTest` pins: spent±rearm, no-target-at-home after/before first contact, in/out of window, target-holds, forward-holds, range boundary inclusive, determinism). `make.ps1 test` YAML lint: the 422 errors are ALL pre-existing (map cordons + unrelated rules-field items e.g. `EjectionSurvivalRate`/`being-captured`); the field-existence lint did NOT flag the new trait fields ⇒ they are recognized. No in-game/autotest run (hard rule + explicitly forbidden this window); the evac cadence/K:D effect is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game and needs a benchmark before trusting the tuning (500-tick window / 60-cell range / 12-cell home radius are first-guess values).

## 2026-07-31 — AdaptiveProduction combat-buy routing hazard is LIVE on @experimental NATO (not BRICS): `unitProducers[0]` is the DISABLED `@russia.fixedwing` twin, so every counter-buy vanished — fixed with the canonical `FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault` seam, gated so @stable stays byte-identical (auto/adaptive-routing)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI production (disabled-but-constructed producer twins — `TraitsImplementing<IBotRequestUnitProduction>` returns condition-DISABLED twins in construct/merge order; routing to `[0]` lets a disabled twin swallow requests so `pending` climbs while `alive` stays 0; fix routes to the first ENABLED producer via `Exts.FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault`, `AdaptiveProduction` gated default-off `RouteToEnabledProducer`). Verified `AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:93/:128/:269`, `AdaptiveRoutingMath.cs:35`, `Exts.cs:596-606`, `McvManagerBotModule.cs:117`, `HarvesterBotModule.cs:158`. Call-site line refs drifted +12 (:242/:332 → :254/:356).]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Closed the "NOTE (out of scope)" the `auto/tecn-priority-fix` entry below left open (line ~15): `AdaptiveProductionBotModule` carried the IDENTICAL `[0]`-routing hazard on the COMBAT path that a3bceb5a fixed on the capture path. Base `main` @ `a3bceb5a`. Verified LIVE before fixing, then fixed only where live.

- **VERDICT: LIVE on @experimental NATO; a no-op on @experimental BRICS; must stay frozen on @stable.** `unitProducers = PlayerActor.TraitsImplementing<IBotRequestUnitProduction>()` returns ALL `UnitBuilderBotModule` twins incl. condition-disabled ones, in trait CONSTRUCT order. Construct order preserves YAML/merge order among same-type traits (`ActorInfo.TraitsInConstructOrder` seeds `resolved` from source order; all UnitBuilder twins share one dependency profile so they never reorder relative to each other). Merge order = mod.yaml Rules load order: `ai.yaml` (`:622` russia.fixedwing, `:661` russia.heli, `:681` america.fixedwing, `:715` america.heli) THEN `ai-america.yaml` (`:7` america.normal, `:54` america.experimental) THEN `ai-russia.yaml`. So `unitProducers[0]` = **`UnitBuilderBotModule@russia.fixedwing`** on EVERY player.
  - On @experimental **NATO**: `[0]`=russia.fixedwing has `RequiresCondition: enable-ai-any && player.brics` → `player.brics` is a never-granted condition on a NATO player (`player.yaml:249-257`, granted by prerequisite/faction), so the twin is CONSTRUCTED-but-`IsTraitDisabled`. Its `BotTick` never runs (`ModularBot.cs:96` ticks only enabled modules), so the legacy `foreach(unitProducers){ RequestUnitProduction; break; }` (`AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:242`, pre-fix) dropped every counter-buy into a dead queue; `RequestedProductionCount` sums that stuck queue so `alreadyRequested>=2` (`:239`) then wedged re-issue. **Hazard confirmed live.**
  - On @experimental **BRICS**: `[0]`=russia.fixedwing is ENABLED (`enable-ai-any && player.brics` both true), so the legacy path already landed on an enabled twin (the single-name `BuildUnit(bot,name)` overload services a ground unit from the aircraft builder by resolving the actor's OWN queue). `FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault` returns that same `[0]` → **byte-identical for BRICS experimental.**
- **`AdaptiveProductionBotModule` is NOT experimental-only — it has `@stable.america`/`@stable.russia` twins (`ai.yaml:873/:882`), so the shared `BotTick` code runs for @stable too.** On @stable NATO the legacy path ALSO routes to disabled `[0]` (currently loses the buy) — but @stable is the frozen benchmark reference and MUST stay bit-identical, so the fix could NOT change routing unconditionally.
- **FIX (mirrors a3bceb5a's "route to the first accepting/enabled producer", reusing the canonical in-repo seam).** McvManager (`McvManagerBotModule.cs:117`) and Harvester (`HarvesterBotModule.cs:158`) ALREADY route via `Exts.FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault()` — AdaptiveProduction was the sole module still on the broken `[0]` shape. New default-off `AdaptiveProductionBotModuleInfo.RouteToEnabledProducer`; `SelectUnitProducer()` delegates the index choice to new pure `AdaptiveRoutingMath.SelectProducerIndex(enabled[], routeToEnabled)` — off ⇒ index 0 verbatim (frozen), on ⇒ first `IsTraitEnabled()` producer. Both call sites (`:242` counter-buy, `:332` SR-defense) rewired through it. Set `RouteToEnabledProducer: true` ONLY on the two `@experimental` AdaptiveProduction twins (`ai.yaml:463/:494`); the `@stable` twins omit it.
- **Byte-identity reasoning.** `@stable.america`/`@stable.russia` omit `RouteToEnabledProducer` (→ `false` → `SelectProducerIndex` returns 0 = the exact legacy `unitProducers[0]`, even when `[0]` is disabled — the frozen lost-buy behaviour is preserved). The SR-defense call site is additionally already `SupplyRouteDefenseEnabled`-gated (experimental-only, off on @stable), so @stable never enters it. No RNG added; `SelectProducerIndex` is a single ordered walk over a construct-order array, `IsTraitEnabled` reads a condition token — no dict/hash enumeration feeds the decision.
- **Verification.** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **622/622** (617 baseline + 5 `AdaptiveRoutingMathTest` pins: frozen-always-0 incl. disabled-`[0]`, first-enabled-skips-disabled, `[0]`-enabled no-op, none-enabled → −1, empty/null → −1). No in-game/autotest run (hard rule) — the "counter-buys now actually build on @experimental NATO" behaviour is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game.

## 2026-07-31 — TECN priority deadlock RESOLVED: the request was landing on a condition-DISABLED UnitBuilder twin (its BotTick never runs), NOT dropped by a busy queue — pending=82/alive=0 was a routing bug, fixed by routing to the first ENABLED producer + peek-don't-pop delivery + an in-flight cap (auto/tecn-priority-fix)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI production (same disabled-twin routing paragraph — the accept/reject handshake half: `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction.RequestPriorityUnitProduction` returns `false` when `IsTraitDisabled`, and `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule` routes to the first twin that accepts; peek-don't-pop priority drain makes delivery non-lossy). Verified `UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:154-164`, `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:683-694`, `ModularBot.cs:96`.]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Confirmed and fixed the supply-side deadlock the `auto/tecn-supply` merge (`f9da1860`) left behind. Base `main` @ `02667dfb`. The prior entry's "REPORT DISCREPANCY" bullet guessed the pending-gate deadlock could only happen "unless the target `unitProducers[0]` instance is perpetually paused/never-ticked (unconfirmed)" — this pass **confirms exactly that**, but the cause is condition-DISABLE, not pausing, and the task's stated "busy-queue drop" mechanism is NOT what the benchmark exhibited.

- **VERIFIED ROOT CAUSE — the priority request lands on a DISABLED UnitBuilder twin whose `BotTick` never runs.** A player carries many `UnitBuilderBotModule` instances (`ai-america.yaml:7/:54` `.normal`+`.experimental`, `ai-russia.yaml:6/:53`, plus `ai.yaml:622/:661/:681/:715` fixedwing/heli), all but one condition-disabled per game (`RequiresCondition: … && !enable-ai-experimental` vs `enable-ai-experimental`). `TraitsImplementing<IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction>()` returns ALL of them incl. disabled ones; `MaintainTecnFloor` called `priorityProducers[0].RequestPriorityUnitProduction(...)` (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:686`, pre-fix). `ModularBot.cs:96` ticks only enabled modules (`if (t.IsTraitEnabled()) t.BotTick(this)`), but the interface methods are called directly regardless of enabled state. So `[0]` (a disabled twin) accepted the request into its `priorityBuildRequests`, its `BotTick` never drained it, yet its `RequestedProductionCount` kept counting it → the coordinator's `pending` climbed forever while `alive` stayed 0 and no TECN was ever built.
- **PROOF from the raw batch (`tools/autotest/tournament-results/260731_streak_exp_vs_stable0730_tecn_s2combat/match_1_debug.log`):** `tecn-floor-request … alive=0 pending=78→79→80→81→82 priority=True floor=5`, exactly one issue per ~225 ticks, pending increasing by EXACTLY 1 per issue with **nothing ever draining it**. If the enabled UnitBuilder had received it, its 30-tick `BotTick` drain (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:123`) would `RemoveAt(0)` unconditionally (pre-fix) and pending would oscillate near 0, not grow monotonically — so the receiving instance's drain provably never ran ⇒ it was disabled. The 4/10 games that DID field TECN got them from the enabled experimental UnitBuilder's own blind lottery (`UnitsToBuild: tecn.america 500`), independent of the floor lever entirely.
- **The "busy-queue drop" (task's stated diagnosis) is a REAL but SECONDARY latent bug.** `BuildUnit(bot, name)` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:265`) only issues on an empty queue (`FirstOrDefault(q => !q.AllQueued().Any())`) and the drain popped `RemoveAt(0)` even on failure — so even once routing is fixed, a busy Infantry queue would silently discard the popped request. Fixed alongside so delivery is actually reliable.
- **FIX (all @experimental-gated, @stable byte-identical):** (1) `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction.RequestPriorityUnitProduction` now returns `bool`; the UnitBuilder returns `false` when `IsTraitDisabled` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs`), and `MaintainTecnFloor` routes to the FIRST producer that accepts (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs` foreach) → the request lands on the enabled twin. (2) Priority drain is **peek-don't-pop**: `if (Count>0 && BuildUnit(head)) RemoveAt(0)` — a busy-queue cycle keeps the request at the head for the next free slot; the drain still runs before the FIFO/lottery so it PRE-EMPTS the next free slot; in-progress builds are never cancelled. (3) `CaptureSupplyMath.ShouldRequestTecn` gains an in-flight CAP (`if (pending >= floor) return false;` after the staleTicks<=0 frozen guard) → pending bounded to [0, floor]; the staleness re-issue is now a backstop that can't duplicate, since peek-don't-pop makes the in-flight request non-lossy.
- **Byte-identity reasoning.** `@stable.tecn` (`ai.yaml:801`) sets `TecnFloor:1` but omits `TecnRequestPriority` (→ ordinary path, never the priority routing change) and `TecnRequestStaleTicks` (→ `staleTicks=0`, the cap sits after the frozen-guard return, so `ShouldRequestTecn` is bit-identical). The priority-drain block only executes when `priorityBuildRequests` is non-empty, which requires a `TecnRequestPriority` caller (experimental-only) → the block is skipped for normal/rush/turtle/@stable. The ordinary FIFO drain (`:135-140`) is untouched (it ignores `BuildUnit`'s new bool return and still removes unconditionally). Zero RNG added.
- **NOTE (out of scope): `AdaptiveProductionBotModule` has the same `[0]` routing shape** (`AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:242/:332` `foreach up … break`) for its combat buys; if `[0]` is disabled its combat requests also vanish (the queue was kept busy by the enabled UnitBuilder's own lottery, not by these). Not touched — it is the combat path, not the TECN lever, and changing it would alter @experimental combat behaviour.
- **Verification.** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **617/617** (615 baseline + 2 net new `CaptureSupplyMathTest` cap pins; 1 existing staleness test rewritten to the partial-floor case the cap still allows). No in-game/autotest run (hard rule; measurement dispatched separately) — the fix is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot S2 game.

## 2026-07-31 — TECN capture-supply guarantee: the S2-loss "TecnFloor deadlock" is really a queue-slot STARVATION, not a pending-gate deadlock; all four fixes gate off NEW default-off Info fields because @stable.tecn ALSO sets TecnFloor:1 (auto/tecn-supply)

> **[rejected: superseded by the `auto/tecn-priority-fix` entry above — this entry's "REPORT DISCREPANCY" bullet guessed the deadlock could only come from a paused/never-ticked producer; the later entry CONFIRMED exactly that, but the cause is condition-DISABLE, not pausing, and it fixed the routing. The durable routing fact is promoted via that entry → architecture.md §AI production; the per-feature byte-identity gating (`@stable.tecn` also sets `TecnFloor:1`, so each fix keys off a NEW default-off Info field reproducing the frozen path) is an application of the already-promoted shared-trait rule (architecture.md §Adding a behavioural field to a trait shared by both bot profiles).]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Implemented the four coupled capture-supply fixes on `auto/tecn-supply`, base `main` @ `e3ba32f9`, driven by `WORKSPACE/recon/260731-loss-analysis-exp-vs-stable0730.md` (Experimental fielded ZERO TECN in 6/10 S2 games → captured nothing → lost; each held oilb ≈33k dwarfs 2–8k combat). New pure math `CaptureSupplyMath` (floor scaling + re-request predicate) + `CaptureFanoutMath` (distinct-target selection), consumed by `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule`; new `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction` dormant seam on `UnitBuilderBotModule`.

- **The byte-identity gate is NOT `TecnFloor > 0` — `@stable.tecn` sets `TecnFloor: 1` too (`ai.yaml:813`), so it runs `MaintainTecnFloor` every scan.** The recon/task framing ("set only on @experimental, so @stable stays byte-identical") is right about the *value* but the master gate had to be per-feature: every new behaviour keys off a NEW Info field whose default reproduces the current code path (`TecnRequestStaleTicks=0`, `ScaleTecnFloorToPois=false`, `TecnRequestPriority=false`, `CaptureFanoutEnabled=false`), and `@stable.tecn` omits all four → its `MaintainTecnFloor` and PoiMap capture pass are bit-for-bit unchanged. `CaptureSupplyMath.ShouldRequestTecn(floor,alive,pending,tick,last,staleTicks<=0)` is provably the exact frozen `alive+pending<floor` gate (NUnit-pinned across the whole boundary), and `EffectiveFloor(scaleEnabled=false,…)` returns the static floor verbatim.
- **REPORT DISCREPANCY — the `:619` "pending≥1 suppresses re-requests forever" deadlock is NOT reproducible from a static read; the confirmed starvation is a lost queue-slot race.** `UnitBuilderBotModule.BotTick` pops `queuedBuildRequests.FirstOrDefault()` and `Remove`s it **unconditionally** every `FeedbackTime=30` ticks (`:118-123`, whether or not the build succeeded), so `RequestedProductionCount` (= the coordinator's `pending`) returns to 0 within ~30 ticks — it cannot sit at 1 for 18k ticks unless the target `unitProducers[0]` instance is perpetually paused/never-ticked (unconfirmed). The mechanism that IS mechanically confirmed (and matches the older `260720_tecn_availability_cycle2.md` recon): the single-name `BuildUnit(bot,name)` overload only builds on an **empty** Infantry queue (`FirstOrDefault(q => !q.AllQueued().Any())`), which combat production keeps busy, so the popped TECN request is **silently dropped** and the TECN never enters production. So the true failure is *combat out-competing for the queue slot*, targeted by change (c); change (a)'s tick-based re-issue additionally covers the framed pending-deadlock if it does occur via a paused producer.
- **(c) A dormant priority-request seam keeps the SHARED UnitBuilder byte-identical.** The experimental bot has no ground UnitBuilder of its own — ground units (incl. TECN) come through the shared `IBotRequestUnitProduction` FIFO on the `enable-ai-any` aircraft UnitBuilders, used by @stable too. Adding an experimental-only YAML field to those instances would leak into @stable. Instead the priority lives on the REQUESTER side: new `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction` + a `priorityBuildRequests` list drained before the FIFO and the blind lottery, populated ONLY when the coordinator opts in (`TecnRequestPriority`). @stable.tecn calls the ordinary path → the priority list stays empty → `UnitBuilderBotModule.BotTick` skips the new block → byte-identical. (Not persisted in the save data — transient, re-issued each scan — so the save format is also unchanged.)
- **(b) The floor scales off the SAME PoiMap capture list, counting neutral IncomeStructure/Capture POIs only.** `CountReachableNeutralMoneyPois` filters `OrderedCaptureTargets()` to `Kind==IncomeStructure && Action==Capture && owner==Neutral` (enemy income is defended, SRs aren't money), clamped `[TecnFloor, TecnFloorMax]` → ~1 capturer per free oilb, cap 5. Deterministic integer count over the sorted list.
- **(d) The 2-TECN→1-oilb clustering is CROSS-scan, not within-scan.** `QueueCaptureOrdersFromPoiMap` already fans out within one scan (each iteration removes the assigned capturer, and PoiMap targets are distinct actors). The waste is a newly-idle capturer in a LATER scan being sent to an oilb another capturer is already walking to (still Neutral, so still top of the list; the goal-guard ledger tracks the CAPTURER commitment, not the target). Fix: build the in-flight target-id set from committed capturers' `capture:<id>` objectives and exclude them (`CaptureFanoutMath.SelectDistinctTargets`, ordered walk, membership-only set reads — no hash-order leak).
- **Verification.** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **615/615** (600 baseline + 9 `CaptureSupplyMathTest` + 6 `CaptureFanoutMathTest`). No in-game run / autotest (hard rule + the fix is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot S2 game) — declared as the later benchmark check, same posture the escort/echelon/standoff levers shipped under.

## 2026-07-31 — Frontier-distance BFS needs no new timer or gate — it rides RecomputePlayer, which IS the Participates gate; standoff push-back reduces to a coordinate-agnostic step count (auto/frontier-standoff)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers (frontier-distance standoff — `DistanceToEnemyFrontier` BFS computed at the tail of `ControlField.RecomputePlayer` so it rides the Participates gate with no new timer; both consumers reduce to `FrontierStandoffMath.RearwardSteps`, one Chebyshev-scaled coarse-cell hop with an explicit off-grid hard-stop; default-off `MinFrontierDistanceCells`). Verified `FrontierStandoffMath.cs:59`, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:280/:407/:1028`, `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:95`. Item B (escort reduction-only `EscortSizingMath.ResolveEscortCount`) is already covered by the promoted escort-sizing entry above.]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Implemented `DistanceToEnemyFrontier` + standoff consumers on `auto/frontier-standoff`, base `main` @ `3975b012`. The follow-on seam after the frontline contour shipped: the front is well-defined data-side (`ControlFieldMath.IsFrontlineEdge`, the enemy-region half-plane), so give standoff units a distance-to-that-region field to hold behind.

- **The BFS is auto-gated to participants by WHERE it runs, not a flag.** `ControlField.RecomputePlayer` is reached ONLY via the `InfluenceStack` participant cursor (`GatherParticipants` → `Participates`), so calling `ComputeFrontierDistance` at the tail of `RecomputePlayer` (after `ApplyAnchors`, when the score grid is final for this cycle) inherits the exact per-player gating + `UpdateInterval` cadence the score field already has — no second timer, no explicit `Participates` check, and @stable/normal never build the array because they never get a `PlayerControl` at all. Mirrors how `LastVerified` piggybacks the same loop (`ControlField.cs`).
- **Distance-to-region is order-independent, so the determinism belt-and-braces is nearly free.** Multi-source BFS seeded row-major from every `Classify==Enemy` cell (score < −GrayBand), 4-connectivity, FIFO `Queue<int>` (cell packed `(gx<<16)|gy`). Because a stored value is an edge-count shortest path, neighbour visit order can never change it — the fixed neighbour order is insurance, not correctness. Bounded work: the flood stops expanding a ring once `nd >= MaxFrontierDistanceCells` (default 64), so unreached cells (and EVERY cell when no enemy region exists) keep the cap as the "far" sentinel. `FrontierDistanceAt` returns that same sentinel for no-field/off-grid ⇒ a consumer reads "far behind the front" ⇒ zero push ⇒ byte-identical until the field is populated.
- **Both standoff consumers reduce to ONE pure decision: how many rearward steps.** The echelon anchor (WPos, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.PushEchelonBehindFrontier`) and the heli engage cell (CPos, `HelicopterStates.PushHeliBehindFrontier`) live in different coordinate systems, but the decision — walk back along the away-from-target axis in one-coarse-cell hops until frontier distance ≥ min, bounded — is coordinate-agnostic. `FrontierStandoffMath.RearwardSteps(Func<int,int> frontierAtStep, minCells, maxSteps)` takes a caller-built stepper (WPos or CPos), so one NUnit-pinned function serves both; each consumer just multiplies the returned step count by its own step vector. Step budget `min+2` is enough to lift a distance-0 anchor clear yet is a hard cap — a walk-back, never a free search (task constraint). The heli's rear bearing is `squadLeader − target` (well-defined even when the engage cell == the target, which the WPos echelon's `anchor − target` is not — echelon anchors are already offset behind the screen so that case doesn't arise there).
- **Byte-identity gating differs by consumer, both default-off.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule@experimental` is a per-profile instance ⇒ a bare `MinFrontierDistanceCells > 0` gate on the experimental block suffices (the `controlField` resolution was broadened to `StrategicRepointEnabled || MinFrontierDistanceCells > 0`; the push is additionally `&& controlField != null`). The heli module gate is `standoff ? info.MinFrontierDistanceCells : 0` then `> 0` + `control.HasField(player)`, so it can't fire without the standoff loop it rides. Both default 0 in C#; enabled `4` (≈8 map cells) only in the two `@experimental` YAML blocks.
- **Item B — the escort reduction-only guarantee was live in an inline `Math.Min`, now extracted + pinned.** `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.DispatchEscort`'s tier→count mapping (None→0, Light→`Math.Min(want, LightEscortSize)`, Full→want) is the actual "the lever never RAISES an escort" guarantee but was untested. Extracted verbatim to `EscortSizingMath.ResolveEscortCount` (byte-identical rewire) with an exhaustive NUnit pin (`ResolveEscortCount(want,tier,light) ≤ want` across all tiers × sizes) — so a future mis-tune of `LightEscortSize` above `EscortSize` provably still can't enlarge an escort.
- **A rearward step scaled to one coarse cell EUCLIDEAN under-advances a diagonal — use max-norm.** The grid is indexed per-axis (CellSize map cells per coarse cell in EACH of X and Y), so to cross into an adjacent coarse cell along a 45° bearing you must advance CellSize map cells in BOTH axes — a step of one coarse-cell *Euclidean length* only moves ~0.7 coarse cells per axis, so consecutive hops re-read the same coarse grid cell and the walk clears fewer cells than the budget implies (worse in integer CELL space, where the toward-zero division collapses a diagonal to a 1-map-cell step). Fix: scale by the MAX component (Chebyshev), so the dominant axis advances exactly one coarse cell and every hop lands on a distinct coarse cell regardless of direction. Both consumers walk in WPos (sub-cell) via the shared `FrontierStandoffMath.RearwardStep`.
- **Off-grid mid-walk reads as "clear" — the boundary must be a hard stop, not a frontier value.** `FrontierDistanceAt` returns the FAR sentinel for off-grid cells (the same value a populated-but-enemy-free field gives), so a rearward hop that steps off the playable edge looks "behind the front" and the walk would accept a position past the map edge. `RearwardSteps` therefore takes an explicit `onGrid` predicate and never counts a hop that fails it — halting at the boundary with the last on-grid hop — rather than trusting the sampler to signal off-grid.
- **Verification.** Build clean (0 errors); NUnit **600/600** (7 `FrontierStandoffMathTest` incl. the diagonal-distinctness + boundary pins, 2 `ControlFieldMathTest` BFS pins, 1 `EscortSizingMathTest` reduction-only pin). No in-game run (game running on this machine; window focus protected) — the actual "artillery/helis now hold behind the front" behaviour is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game, so it is a declared later in-game/benchmark check, same posture the echelon/escort levers shipped under.

## 2026-07-31 — Escort right-sizing composes as a REDUCTION-ONLY lever with the existing contest-aware BUMP; capture escorts are never ledger-committed so freed units need no redirection system (auto/escort-sizing)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers beyond D/E/F — reduction-only escort tiering (`EscortSizingMath` over the ControlField ring + GroundDanger + SR distance), composes with the contest bump, escorts never ledger-committed. Verified `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:188/:987-994/:1018-1042`, `EscortSizingMath.cs`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Implemented believed-threat escort tiering on `auto/escort-sizing`, base `main` @ `bb4911a5`. Motivating report (spectating): the @experimental bot walks a full escort (+ the contest-aware bump to 4) to EVERY derrick and the party idles at the POI until capture completes — even oil derricks in our own rear next to our SR where there is zero threat. New `EscortSizingMath.Resolve` (`EscortSizingMath.cs`) buckets a target None/Light/Full from the ring-averaged ControlField score, DangerFieldLayer.GroundDanger, and distance-from-own-SR; consumed by `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.DispatchEscort`.

- **The two escort levers must compose, and the clean way is to make the new one REDUCTION-ONLY.** `ContestAwareSupportEnabled` (shipped, ON in `ai.yaml`) already BUMPS the escort 2→4 for a contested derrick and pre-summons defenders earlier. Naively replacing the escort-count decision with a full None/Light/Full tier would fight that shipped bump. Instead the new lever only ever LOWERS `wantEscort`: it is applied inside `if (Info.EscortTierSizingEnabled && !contested)` so a target `IsContestedNeighbourhood` already flagged keeps its (possibly bumped) escort untouched, and within the not-contested branch the tier can only set 0 (None) or `Math.Min(wantEscort, LightEscortSize)` (Light). Net: contested→bump (old lever), safe→shrink (new lever), and they never disagree because the `!contested` guard partitions the targets. Consequence: within `!contested` the helper's `Full` branch is structurally unreachable from the module (contested already caught danger>threshold and any ring-enemy), so `Full` exists only as the pure-helper contract/tests — intentional, documented in the module comment.
- **Freed combat units need NO redirection system — capture escorts were never reserved past the tick.** `DispatchEscort` issues a single grouped `AttackMove` and adds the recruits only to a tick-local `alreadyRecruited` set; it does NOT commit them in the `PoiGoalGuard` ledger (unlike the TECN capturer itself, which IS committed). So an escort that reaches the POI and goes idle is immediately re-eligible for `PoiOffensiveBotModule`'s axis recruitment and for later captures (both filter on `IsIdle` + not-ledger-committed). Therefore "combat units freed from safe-POI babysitting go to the offense" is automatic the moment we simply DON'T recruit them for a None-tier target — no new tasking path, matching the brief's "simplest correct integration wins." (The physical lingering-at-the-POI is also self-correcting: the offense claims them while idle.)
- **A neutral derrick's OWN cell is NOT anchor-floored, but its enemy-owned sibling's IS — so sample the RING for both.** ControlField's `ApplyAnchors` stamps enemy anchors only for BELIEVED-ENEMY site structures; a NEUTRAL derrick (the common early capture) is neither self nor enemy, so its own grid cell reads ambient control, while an enemy-OWNED derrick's cell is floored to ≈ −800 out to `AnchorRadiusCells`. To read a single consistent "who surrounds this target" signal across both target kinds, `ResolveEscortTier` samples the 8-neighbour ring at `AnchorRadiusCells+1` (reusing the module's existing `RingDirections`), exactly the Stage-F balance-of-power geometry — one cell past the floored disc.
- **Distance-from-own-SR is already carried fog-legally by `ScoredPoi.DistanceCells`** ("from the perspective player's own SR", `PoiMap.cs:66`) — threaded through `IssueCaptureOrder`→`DispatchEscort` from the PoiMap capture path. The legacy no-PoiMap path passes `-1` (unknown), which the pure helper treats as failing the near-SR gate, so a lone technician is never sent on a guess. `PoiMap.OwnSupplyRoute(perspective)` is the public seam if a future non-PoiMap caller needs the SR actor directly.
- **Byte-identity + verification.** New flag `EscortTierSizingEnabled` default false on the engine class; only `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule@experimental.tecn` sets it (the `@stable.tecn` twin lacks the fields → false → `DispatchEscort` short-circuits to the exact pre-change `wantEscort` and never resolves a field). Threaded `distanceFromSRCells` is consumed only inside the disabled block. Zero RNG. Build clean (0 errors); NUnit **589/589** (7 new `EscortSizingMathTest` pins: full-first on danger/deep-enemy, none-boundaries, distance gate on/off, unknown-distance→light). YAML lint unchanged at **422** (all pre-existing map-cordon errors; none reference the AI module or the new keys). No behavioral autotest — the escort sizing is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game (the single-human AUTOTEST harness can't form the capture pool), same posture the capture-contest / echelon levers shipped under.

## 2026-07-30 — Defence-in-depth echelon: the fires standoff anchors on the TARGET, so it never guarantees the screen is between artillery and the enemy — the fix anchors on the OWN SCREEN instead (auto/echelon)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI configuration (fires standoff is TARGET-relative; default-off `EchelonPositioning` holds IndirectFire behind the MainBattle screen centroid by range surplus, falls back to target-standoff when no screen exists). Verified `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:255/:1007-1016`, `EchelonMath.cs`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Implemented role-aware echelon positioning on `auto/echelon`, base `main` @ `314631d3`. Motivating report (user spectating): artillery gets sent alone to the front and dies. `FiresStandoff` (PIPELINE item 11) is already ON in `@experimental` — so why does artillery still die at the front?

- **The item-11 fires standoff is TARGET-relative, not SCREEN-relative — that is the whole gap.** `FiresStandoffMath.StandoffAnchor(target, unit, …)` (`FiresStandoffMath.cs:80`) places the piece at `maxRange − margin` from the axis TARGET, on the bearing from the target back toward the piece's CURRENT position. Nothing in that geometry references the friendly screen: if the piece is anchored off to a flank, or advances ahead of a slower `MainBattle` group, or the target is a forward POI the screen has not yet reached, the standoff ring can sit the piece level with or ahead of the line — exposed. The standoff solved "hold at weapon range and back off if the target closes"; it did NOT solve "stay behind your own shield." Those are different reference frames (enemy vs. own line).
- **The clean seam is the SAME peel-off loop.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule.OrderFiresStandoff` already separates `IndirectFire`-role pieces from the axis and gives each its own AttackMove destination; the remainder (`groupUnits`) IS the `MainBattle` screen. So the echelon needs no new tasking path — it only swaps the per-piece anchor computation: when `EchelonPositioning` is on AND a screen exists, anchor `depth` behind the screen CENTROID offset directly away from the target (`EchelonMath.EchelonAnchor`), where `depth = max(EchelonMinDepth, (ownMaxRange − screenRange) + EchelonBuffer)` and `screenRange` = the longest live weapon reach among the screen units.
